STATE ; Bihar, INDUSTRY ; Coah 

Dated, Dhanbud, the 12lli January, 1990 
AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour in exercise 
of the powers conferred on them under .‘^cetion 10(l)(d) of 
the I. D. Act, 1947 has referred the following dispute to 
this Tribunal for adjudication vide their Order No. 1.-24012/ 
l/87-D,I(B) dated, the 20th July, 1987. 

SCHEDULE 

'"Whether the action of the Managemeni of P.R.S.P. 
Kankanee of Sijua Area No. V of M/s. Bharat 
Coking Coal Ltd., Dhanbad in not placing Sri Mohd. 
Jrfan Ansari, Auto Electrician Excavation in E.xca- 
vation Group D with eli'eet from 11-2-1982 i.s justi¬ 
fied ? If not, to what relief the concerned workman 
is entitled 7" 

The case of the workman is that Shri Mb. Irfan An.sari 
was appointed as an Auto Electrician (Trainee) vide appoint¬ 
ment letter dated 14-3-81 in Group E. He was to be under 
training for one year and theie.iflcr he was to be given 
his scale of pay of Excavation Group as Auio Electrician. 
He was posted to Nichltpur O.C.P. vide order dated 14-4-81. 
After he completed the period of training he was rcgulariscil 
as Auto Electrician in Group H with effect from 11-4-82. 
The rcgularisalion of the conccrnedl workman in Excavation 
Group E was not proper and he shou'd have been regularised 
in Group D excavation ns an Auto Electrician with effect 
from 11-4-82. Excavation Group E is given to Auto Elec¬ 
trician Helper. The concerned workman demanded his regii- 
Inrisatioii in Excavation Group D and the wages of Group D 
in April, 1982. He again demanded by letter dated. 1-11-85 
for regularising him in Group D and payment of the wages 
of Group D. Thereafter the union of the concerned woik- 
man also made a demand for giving Excavation Group D 
and its scale of pay to the concerned workman and also his 
promotion to Excavation Group C. When the management 
did not pay any heed the union raised an industrial dispute 
before the ALCfC), Dhanbad and on failure of the concilia- 
lion the present reference has been made for adjudication. 
It is prayed by the workman that the concerned workman 
should be given Excavation Group D with ellecl from 11-4-82 
along with the difference of wages of Group E & D and 
that he should be promoted to Excavation Group C. 

The case of the management is that the workman have 
been demanding promotion which is the function of the 
manaperaerit and cannot be claimed as a matter of right. It 


is .staled that the concerned v orkman docs not pos.sess the 
reipilsite qualification and experience for being promoted as 
an Auto Electrician iii the Escasation Ciroup D. The con¬ 
cerned woikman was temrural'iK appointed tm probation for 
a period ol one year by letter dated 14-3-81 and he joined 
on 10-4-81. He w;is appointed as an Aulo Electrician Exca¬ 
vation (Trainee) m Group E and as posted at Nichitpur 
Tetulmai-y Project by letter dated 14-4-81. He was regula¬ 
rised vide letter dated 12-5-83. The concerned workman does 
not possess ITl certificate. The job performance of the con¬ 
cerned workman is equivalent to the job of Auto Electrician 
Helper, M/s, BCCL has introduced cadre scheme for every 
cadre and no promotion is possible in violation of the cadre 
scheme of the management. It was not a condition of his 
nppointment that he should be placed ns Auto Electrician 
Excavalioh in Excavation Group D immediately after com- 
p'eiion of one year probation. On the above facts it Is prayed 
that the concerned workman cannot be givfn Group D and 
that he is entitled to no relief. 

The only point for decision in this case is whether the 
concerned workman should be placed as Auto Electrician 
Excavation in Excavation Group D with effect from 11-2-82. 

The management and the workmen each examined one 
witness in support of their respective case. The documents 
of the management are marked Ext. M l to M-7 and tho 
documents of the workmen are marked Ext. W-1 to W-10, 


The dispute in the case is noiv melted down to the point 
whether the concerned workman should be given the differ¬ 
ence of wages of Group E and Group D from 14-3-82 since 
the management vide its office order dated 26-5-89 ordered 
that the concerned workman Shri Irfan Ansari will gel senio¬ 
rity in Exc.avation Group D with effect from 14-3-82. This 
office order Ext. W-9 was passed during the hearing of this 
reference and as such now there is no dispute about the 
point whether the concerned workman should get E.xcavation 
Group D with effect from 14-3-82 when he hud completed 
one year of his training period. However the office order 
E,\t. W-9 shows that the effective financial benefit will be on 
and from 11-11-86 as approved by Ihe competent authority. 
Thus vide this office order the management ordered not to 
pay the wages of Group D and the difference of wages of 
Group E and Group D from 14-3-82 to 10-11-86. 

As the concerned workman has been given Excnvatioii 
Group D with effect from 14-3-82 all other contiovcrsies 
whether the concerned workman Was entu'ed to Group D 
after one year of his period of training or not no longer 
exists. It will also appear that excavation Group E is given 
to Auto Electrician Helper and Group D is given lo an Aulo 
Electrician. MW 1 Shri R.B.P, Sahi is a Sr. Personnel Officer. 
He has staled in his ems-s-examination that the concerned 
workman was appointed as Auto Electrician Excavation 
trainee and that auto Electricians are in Excavation Group 
D. He ha.s further slated that the concerned workman is 
working regularly and continuoiis'y as Aulo Electrician. It is 
clear therefore that tho concerned woikman was regularly 
working as Aulo Electrician and continuously after the period 
of his training and this fact also will show that the con¬ 
cerned workmen was to be placed in Excavation Group D 
and he should have been paid the sca'c of pay of Exeax'a- 
tion Group D. The management has given no reason in 
Ext. W-9 as to why the eflecti\c date of financial benefit 
was give.n to the concerned workman from 11-11-86. iTie 
evidence of the management itself shows that the concerned 
workman was regularly and continuously working as Auto 
Electrician and as such there is no reason as lo why he 
should not he given the wa.vcs of Excavation Group D with 
effect from 14-3-82. Ext. W ID is an office order dated 
22/23-3-88 which shows that Aulo Electricians who had join¬ 
ed as trainees in 1982 and had completed one year of their 
training in 1983 were given Excavation Gioim ‘D’ in 1983 
as soon as they had completed one year of the training. 
Ext. W-10 shows that the management had given Excavation 
Group D after completion of one year of the period of 
training aijd in their case there is no subsequent date of 
effective date for financial benefit. It will thus appear that 
the case of the concerned workman i.s being discriminated 
pgainst him whereas in the case of others as thowq ip Ext. 



520 the gazette OF INDIA : EEBRUARV 17, 1990/JMAGHA 28, 1911 


W 10 others wore given Excavation Grade D just after com¬ 
pletion of one year of the period of training. In this view 
of the mattei 1 hold that the concerned workman deserves 
the scale of pay of Excavation Group D with effect from 
14-3-82 and that he should be paid the difference of wages 
of Excavation Group E and Excavation Group D from 
14-3-82. 

In the result, I hold that the action of the management 
of P.R.S.P. Kankanec of Sijua Area No. V of M/s, BCGL 
in not placing the concerned workman Shri Md. Irfan Ansari 
Auto Electrician Excavation in Excavation Group D with 
effect from 14-3-82 is not justified. As now the concerned 
workman has been placed in Excavation Group D with effect 
from 14-3-82 there is no need to pass any direction to place 
in Excavation Group D. The management however, is direct¬ 
ed to pay the difference of wages of Excavation Group E 
and Excavation Group D from 14-3-82 to 10-11-86 to tfie 
concerned workman within one month from the date of 
publication of the Award. 

This is my Award. 

T. N. STNHA, Presiding OlHcer 
[No. L-24012(l)/87-D.lV(B)/I'R (Coal-I)] 
K. J. DYV.\ PRASAD, Desk Officer

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour in exercise 
of the powers conferred on them by clause (d) of sub-sectio'h 
<1) and sub-section (2-A) of Section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 has referred the following dispute to 
this Tribunal for adjudication under Order No. L-24012 
(178)/87-D. IV (B), dated 6-3-1988. 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the Management of Chora 
Colliery of M/s. E. C. Ltd., P. O. Bahula, Dislt. 
Burdwan (W. B.) in refusing to pay wages to S/Shri 
Gopal Dome and 18 others as per Annexure-A, 
for the period of forced idleness from 15-10-1986 
to 2-1-1987 is justified ? If not, to what relief are 
the concerned workmen entitled ?”. 

2. Today (5-1-1990) Shri Manoj Mukherjee the leained 
Advocate for the workmen has submitted that he has no 
instruction to proceed with the case. He has prayed for 
passing appropriate order. Shri P. K. Das the learned Ad¬ 
vocate for the management is also present. 

3. As the learned Lawyer for the union has no instruction 
from his clients to proceed with the case, it must be presum¬ 
ed that no dispute exists between the pailie.s. As such I 
have no other alternative but to pass a ‘no dispute’ award 
in this case. Hence a ‘no dispute" award is passed. 

This is my award. 

N. K. SAHA, Presiding Officer.

AWARD 

The Government of India in die Ministry of Labour, in 
e.'vercise of the powers conterred upon them by clause (d) 
of sub-section (1) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), and by their Order No. L12012/ 
897[87-D. 11(A), dated 10th July. (988 have referred the 
following dispute for adjudication by this Tribunal ;— 

‘■Whether the action ot the m.'.nagement of Indian Bank, 
Puri Branch in termiiaating the services of Shri 
Biswanath Sahoo, Sub-staff is justified ? If not, to 
what relief the workman is entitled 

2. It is the ca'" of the second party—workman that he had 
been empancFicu Dy the Manager of the Bank for engage¬ 
ment as substitute sub-staff against available leave vacancies 
and he worked as a sub-staff Peon regularly giving attendance 
every day. On 23-4-1982 on the plea that his services were 
not required in future, he was teiminated. The second party- 
workm.an contended that the plea that he was surplus was 
false, in as much as, after termination of his employment 
the bank has filled up the vacancies of the post of sub-staff 
Peons by recruiting oihsiders. He alleged that termination 
of his services was not a case of termination simplicitor but 
infact, it was retrenchment. He alsO' alleged that the Mana¬ 
gement of the Bank had r‘tpea'edly alleged that his conduct 
and behaviour were thorouglily unsatisfactory, that his ser¬ 
vices weie found detiinieiital and projtid'.ci.il to the inteiest 
of the Bank, that his behaviour was unruly .ind limi he was 
disobedient and iii-vUbordinale a'ul on the basic of those alle¬ 
gations his services were tenidualed without being given any 
opportunity to explain away the allegations against him 


which according to him were false. Such termination of his 
service.s, it is alleged by the .second party—workman, is bad 
in law because it amounts to inlliction of punishment on him 
without any enquiry and in vislalion of the principles of 
natural justice. He demanded reinstatement with back 
wages. 

3. The Management of the Bank took the plea in Us writ¬ 
ten statement that the workman Shri Sahoo had simply been 
empanelled alongwith four others for engagement as substi¬ 
tute on rotation against available leave vacancies. The sec¬ 
ond party Shri Sahoo was being engaged as a substitute sub- 
■staff against leave vacancies. It was stated that'the total 
period for which the second party Shri Sahoo was engaged 
in the Bank was 84 days between 12-5-1980 when he was 
empanelled and 9-4-1982 when his name was cauceiled from 
the Panel. It is the plea of he Management that the second 
party was not required to -work continuously and acquired 
no right to continuous employment. His engagement was 
intermittent and he was being paid wages on daily rate for 
the days he worked against .eave vacancies. The further case 
of the Management of the Bank is that the work and perfor¬ 
mance of the second party Shii Sahoo during his engagement 
as substitute was found iinsatisiactoiy and therefore, it was 
decided not to engage him in future leave vacancies and 
accordingly his name was removed from the Panel an order 
dated 23-4-1982. It is disclosed in the management’s written 
statement that the second party Shri Sahoo filed a suit in the 
Court of the Subordinate Judge, Puri seeking a declaration 
that the action of the Management of the Bank was illegal 
and for decree for Rs. 3,360/- tovv'ai'ds back wages and com¬ 
pensation, The suit was contested. In course of hearing ot 
the suit prayer was made by Shri Sahoo to transfer the suit 
to the Central Administrative Tribunal, Cuttack Branch under 
section 29 of the Administrative Tribunal Act, 1985. The 
Subordinate Judge passed an order on 22-7-1987 recording 
his finding that he had no jurisdiction to try the suit in view 
of the provisions of the Administrative Tribunal Act, 1985 
and ordered return of the plaint to the Centra! Administrative 
Tribunal. Thereafter the second party approached the Assis¬ 
tant Labour Commissioner (Central), Bhubane.swar lor inter¬ 
vention and the Asst. Labour Commissioner (Central), Bhu¬ 
baneswar wrongly accepted and admitted the same to conci¬ 
liation. In the written statement of the Management of the 
Bank it is stated that there was neither any appointment to 
the post so far as the second paity Shri Sahoo was concerned 
and there was no removal Irorn any post. The name of Shu 
Sahoo was merely removed from the panel of the vvaiteis 
for short time engagement. The employer—employee rela¬ 
tionship between the Management of the Bank and the sec¬ 
ond party was only limited to the spells of his engagement 
as a substitute and not beyond that. 

in an additional written statement the Mtmagement of the 
Bank referred to the Administrative instructions issued by the 
Bank from time to time regard.ing engagement of persons 
during leave vacancies of sub-staff and on the basis theieof 
it was contended that the maximum age limit for final engage¬ 
ment of Persons during leave vacancies of sub-staff shall 6e 
30 years and such empanelled Persons can not be employed 
further against leave vacancies on attaining the age of 30 
years. It was also contended on the basis thereof that the 
second party Shri Sahoo’s date ot birth is 2-8-1952 and there¬ 
fore, he was entitled for further engagement against leave 
Vacancies only till 1-8-1982 and not beyond. 

4. On the pleadings of the parlies, the following issues were 
framed :— 

(1) If the second party w'as a ‘workman’ under the 
First Party-Management within the meaning of 
Section 2(s) of the Industiial Disputes Act, 1947 ? 

(2) If the dispute that has been referred for adjudication 
is not an industrial dispute between the First Pafty- 
Mainigement and the second P.iriy 

(3) If there lias not been any termination of employ¬ 
ment of the second party by the First Party—i 
Management as alleged 




526 THE GAZETTE OF 1NDE\ : FEBRUARY 17, 1990/i^E\GHA 28, 1911 


[Part 1I -Sec. 3 (ii) ] 


(4) If the termination of cmploynienc of the second 
party Shri Biswanath Sahoo by the First Part> — 
Management was an act of retrenchment or an act 
of discharge simpliciter ? 

(5) If such termination of employment of the second 
party—workman is legal and/or justified ? 

(6) To what relief, if any, the second party—workman 
is entitled ? 

5. ISSUE NO. 1 ; 

It is’ffle plea of the First Party—Management that the 
second party Shri Sahoo has simp'y teen empanelled to be 
engaged as a substitute Peon against leave vacancies as and 
when required and that too in rotation with such ctner empa¬ 
nelled persons and therefore he was not a "workman’ within 
the meaning of the Industrial Disputes Act. Section 2(s) of 
the Industrial Disputes Act defines ‘workman’ as “any peison 
(including an apprentice) employed in any industry to do any 
manual, unskilled, skihed, technical, operational, clerical or 
supervisory work for hire oi- reward, whether the terms of 
employment by express, implied and for the piupose of any 
proceeding under this Act m relation to an industrial dispute, 
includes any such person who lias been dismissed, discharged 
or retrenched in connection with, or as a consequence of that 
dispute, or whose dismissal, discharge or retrenchment has 

led that dispute,.Po appreciate the question as to 

whether the second party Shri Sahoo was a ’workman’ under 
the Management of the Fust Party—Management, before 
going through the oral evidence, we may look into the ap¬ 
pointment letter Ext. 1 tlated 9th May. 1980. This letter 
was issued by the Manager of the Bank, Puri branch to the 
second party—workman. The subject of the letter has been 
mentioned as “Appointment of Temporary Sub-staff in our 
office.” It is stated in this letter that the Bank intended to 
appoint a few sub-staff purely on temporary basis to work 
in place a permanent sub-staff who goes on leave or on duty 
to other places. It is further mentioned in the said letter 
that the name of the second party had been sponsored by the 
local employment exchange for interview which was schedule 
to be held at the Office of the Bank on 14-5-1980. The 
second party was requested in that letter to appear at the 
interview alongwith his certificate in original. Ext. 2 is the 
termination letter dated 23-4-1982 which vras issued to the 
second party workman by the Manager of the Bank in which 
it was stated that under instnictions from the Zonal Oflice. 
Calcutta his name have been removed from the panel of 
temporary sub-stalT maintained in the Bank. He was also 
informed that his services will not be required in future. A 
notice was sent by the second jiarty to the First Parly 
through an Advocate purported to be under section 80, Civil 
Procedure Code to which ,n leply was sent by tlie First Party 
through its Advocate, which has been admitted in this pro¬ 
ceeding and marked as Ext. 3. In this reply it has been 
categorically stated that the second party bad been engaged 
at the relevant point of time purely on temporary basis and 
therefore, his case for being considered for permanent emp¬ 
loyment would not arise. It was also mentioned that the 
second party had no legal right for being absorbed in the per¬ 
manent cadre being a temporary employee and being engaged 
on daily wage basis at the relevant time. Tn the reply it was 
also mentioned that the Bank was thoroughly dissatisfied with 
the conduct and behaviour of the second party even during 
his temporary spells of engagement at the relevant point of 
time and the second party did not mend It's conduct and 
attitude inspite of repeated reminder given to him. Since 
his conduct was absolute'y unsatisfactory and he was found 
disobedient and insubordinate, inspite of his seniority in the 
panel of temporary staff Shri Sahoo could not be taken to 
the permanent staff. In the reply it was clearly staled that 
on account of such condoct Shri Sahoo was rightly dis- 
empanelled by the Bank. In the reply sent by the Manager 
of the branch Bank to the Asst. Labour Commissioner (Cen¬ 
tral), Bhubaneswar, on 19th October, 1987 (Ext. 4). it was 
stated that Shri Sahoo was beine enijagcd bv (he Bank .'.painst 
temporary leave vacancies of peiro.nnent stib-uap' ns per the 
Bank’s norms and it was further state.l llint tliere was never 
any regular relationship of emnloyer ana employee hetwe'c'n 
the Bank and the second party Shri Sahoo. Ext. 5 is a letter 
dated 25th January, 1982 sent by the Branch Manager of the 


First Party Bank to the Zonal Manager of the Bank at 
Calcalia. In this letter it was mentioned that the second 
party Shri Sahoo was one of the members of the captioned 
Panel maintained by the Puri OlTice and he was appointed 
temporarily from time to time. His performance of duty and 
behaviour were found to be far from salislactory and as such, 
it had been decided not In empiov him any fnriher. la tJiis 
letter confirmation was sought for from the Zonal Manager 
and by Ext. 6 the Zonal Manager directed that the second 
party need not be engaged in future leave vacancies and his 
name be removed from the panel of temporary sub-staff after 
duly informing him. In the written sTilement—Ext. 7 filed 
by the Management of the Bank in the suit filed by the sec¬ 
ond party Shri Sahoo in tlie Court of the Subordinate Judge, 
Puri seeking a declaration that the action of the Manage¬ 
ment of the Bank was bad and claiming back wages and 
compensation, the Management took the plea that the rela¬ 
tionship between the Plaintiff (the second party Shri Sahoo) 
and the defendant (the First Party Management, namely, 
the Indian Bank) was pmeiy one of master and seivant and 
as such, the suit was not competent. It was also stated in 
the said written statement that Shri Saiioo was engaged at 
the relevant point of lime purely on temporary basis termi¬ 
nable at any lime without notice. E.vt. 8 is a copy of the 
/'and confaindig the name of Shri Sahoo and four others 
and Shri Sahoo's position was No. 1 in the said Panel. 


6. M. W. 1, who vvas the Branch Manager of the Puri 
Branch of the Indian Bank from December, 1979 to January, 
1982 stated that the local employment Exchange, Puri had 
been requested by him to furnish a list of twenty uve candi¬ 
dates to be selected to work as sub-staff on temporary basis 
against leave vacancies. 'I he Employment Exchange sent a 
list out of which five candidates were selected to be em¬ 
panelled for such temporary appointment against leave 
vacancies. Sri Sahoo was one of them. Such empanelled 
persons were engaged whenever leave vacancy arose. One 
person out of the list was appointed against such vacancy 
and after the permanent incumbent returns on expiry of his 
leave, he is disengaged. He stated that the second party 
was disengaged because his behaviour was not found to be 
satisfactory and on 23-4-82 he was disengaged and disem- 
panelled from the list. He also stated that out of the 
select list Ext. B in which the second party Sri Sahoo had 
been placed at No. 1 position, Sri Puma Chandra Patra 
and J„axmidhar Bhoi, who were juniors to him have been 
appointed against regular posts. The second paity Sri Saboo 
examined as W.W.l stated that he was engaged daily in 
the bank. 


7. From the evidence on records it appears that the 
Management of the Bank obtained the names of the candi¬ 
dates from the local Employment Exchange, held an inter¬ 
view and selected five out of the candidates sponsored, who 
were empanelled for being appointed against leave vacancies 
as and when lequired. The second party-workmat'. stood 
first in the interview and in the list of empanelment occupi¬ 
ed the No. I position. This being his status, whether it 
could be said that he was a workman within the meaning: 
of the Industrial Disputes Act ? On going through the 
definition of the expression ‘workman’ as giren iii Section 
2(s) of the Industrial Disputes Act, I will have no hesita¬ 
tion to hold that he is a ‘workman’ coming within the 
definition. By being selected in the interview to be em¬ 
panelled ho got a right to be employed against le.'ive 
vacancies and infact, he worked in the Bank agaiiiit leave 
vacancie.s as a sub-staff Peon. The principle of interpreta¬ 
tion of the definition of ‘workman’ and ‘industrial dispute’ 
have been elaborately stated in the case of S. X. Verma. 
Appellant V. Mahesh Chandra and another. Respondent^, 
reported in A.I.R. 1984 S. C. 1462. It has been stated in 
the aforesaid decision that interpretation of the aforesaid 
definitions found in the Jndiistrial Disputes Act siioiild be 
made so as not to whittle down but to advance the ohiect 
of the Act V. ideh is to bi'ing ahoul peace and hainioiiy hef- 
veen labour and managemenl. Nanow and rcstikled mean¬ 
ing must not be given to lire aforesaid definitions so as to 
exclude the labour and nuinagement from the opeialion of 
the Act.

In the facts aiiU ciicunislanccs of the case, 1 would hold 
that the 3"cond party ie a hvciljnaa' v,ithra tho mean n - of 
the l.D. .\Cl. 

ft. IbhUh No, 2.— Hie dispute rcL'cncd foi adiuditation 
relates to termination of services of the second party. In 
other words, it relates to the employment or non employ¬ 
ment of the second party under the Matmgeinent 
of the Bank. His name was removed from die panel 
and he was refused future entploynient by the older seiv- 
ej on him. In the circumstance, there can be no escape 
from the con.'l'.hion that it is an ‘industiial dispute’. 

9. iSbUH Nos. .1, 4 &. 5.—^The second party Sri Salioo 
was called for appeannn; at the interview for appointment 
as lemporany suo-staff in the Bank at Puri as would appeal 
from Kxt. 1. He was intimated by Ext. 2 th.it undei 
instrnclion from the Zonal Office, Calcutta his name had 
been removed fiom the panel of the temporary sub-staff 
maintained by the Bank and further that his services would 
not be rctpiircd in future. In the reply sent by the Bank's 
Advocate to the Advocate of the second party (E.'ii. .1), i: 
has been cleai ly slated that the Bank aulhorilies were 
thorouelily dissatisfied with the conduet and behaviour of 
his client and tiiai his services were detrimental and pi'e- 
judicial to Ihc ir.lt.'esi of the Bank. It was iiienlioned in 
C-\l. 3 that fhc si.cfind parly was found disobedient, insubor¬ 
dinate and unrulv and that inspite of several vvuruiilSs he 
did not mend his ways. In the letter of the .Manager of 
the Branch Bunk at Puri to the Zonal Manaecr, Indian 
Bunk Calcutla-Exl. 5, it was slated that ihc peiformancc 
and behaviour of the second pnty were far from satisfac- 
toiy and os such, he had decided not to employ him any 
furthei' for the smooth running of the Brunch Bank at Puri. 
On the basis of this letter Ext, 5. the Zonal Office, Calcutta 
inlorincd the Manaser of the Branch Bank at Purl that 
he need not cngacc tlie second pa"ty in the future leave 
vacancies and may remove his nainc from tlic Panel of 
lemporary siib-shiff after giving due information to him. 
Tn the said letter advise was given to the Branch Manager, 
Puri branch that he may also Call for fresh list of candi¬ 
date from the local Employment Exchange, if necessary. 


Though by Ext. 2 the second party was informed that his 
name had been icmovcH from the pan**! of temporary ratb- 
staff and that his services would not be required in future 
and there was no mention of any misconduct on his part, 
the evidence adduced in this case, however, reveal that it 
was a case of removal of name of the second p.arly from 
the pancle on grounds of misconduct. The real interdin 
of the Management of the Bank was to get rid of the 


second party whose conduct, according to the Maiiascmcnt 
W'as highly unsati.sfactory. , 

lil- Ihc action ol 'he m.niagcmcnt of the Bank, thus 
amount.s 10 ictnunation of employment, in as much us, he 
was not only removed from the panel but was also denied 
the right to fiilurc appointment as sub-staff Pcoji in the bank, 
which right he had earned by being selected and empanelled 
after the interview. 

11. Question is whether such removal front^Panel or 
termination was justified In this connection, [ mm lefer 
to the decision in lire case of Ajit Kumar Sardar and other.s 
Vrs. Union of India and others reported in 19'K4 Eab, EC. 
woe 1.14 Calcutta whcicin it was held that Jeler'On of 
names of candidates from provisional selected list without 
giving opportunity to them of knowing the basis for such 
deletion is viohu.ve of Articles 14 and 16 of the Constitu¬ 
tion of India. 

Til this particuliu case, there is no evidence to show that 
the second party was ever informed about any inisdonduct 
allegedly conuniltcd by him. Efe was also not informed 
in writing about the dis-satisfaction of the Bank authorities 
so far as his behaviour, attitude and conduct are concerned. 
No explanation v.as called for from him .so as tu enable 
him to explain the position. Tn the circumstance, the order 
as to deletion of his name from the panel and refusing 
him fulurc employment must be held to he Paj being 
violative of Articles 14 and 16 of the Constitution of 
India. Accordingly, I would hold that the order for delet¬ 
ing the name of the .second party from the panel for ap¬ 
pointment as siib-slatf Peon against leave vacancies and 
refusal to him for future appointment against such leave 
vacancies i.s iinjiislified. 

12. ISSUE No. 6.—^Novv coming to the question of relief, 
in the ciicuiustances of the case, T think, the only direc¬ 
tion that can be given is to restore the second patty to the 
position he vvas occupying on 23-4-82. Since we do not 
know as to on v.liich days he would have got employment 
against leave vacancies during the period from 23-4-82 till 
the date of the Award had he continued in the panel, no 
order can be p?,sscd relating to any back wages, 

The rcfe-^Tice is answered accordingly.
